#### Emulator verification (session 39, `tools/emu/verify.py` → `docs/emu_verify.txt`)

`verify.py` loads `name_entry.state` (f2250), plays the tail of the canonical script (`2300:START 2340:DOWN:80`) up to f2460, and checks each call of a routine against a Python model of its REVERSE.md description. The run covers a new game, the start room, the area 0 → 1 move at f2409 and 50 frames in area 1. A call is caught by an exec hook at the entry and checked when PC is back at the pushed return address with S restored (`CallWatch`). Exit code 1 on any failure. **Everything passes** (self-emu only: there is no reference emulator):

| Routine | Calls | What is compared |
|---|---|---|
| `SwitchBank16` `30:C40E` | 2390/2390 | `$8000/$A000` = banks 2n/2n+1 and the shadows `$6E/$6F`; n seen: 00 02-07 09 0D 0E |
| `PlaySound` `30:C125` | 4/4 | `$0100-$0108` after the call vs the model (music dedupe vs `$0102`, `$80-$9F` AND `$7F`, FIFO append, full-queue rules, lock cleared) and Y restored. Ids `00 16 2F 3C`; the full-queue path was not exercised |
| `ObjSpriteDraw` `28:800E` | 65/65 | for all 32 slots: `$05C0/$05E0` and `$0380` b7 from the subtract-with-240-row-wrap rule (incl. `$E8+` = untouched, `<$10` = offscreen, free slot = b7 set), `$07FF` DEC |
| `PaletteBuild` `26:8C10` | 3/3 | `pal_buf` `$6140-$615F` vs the records in banks 2/3, fade `$11`, mask `$07E8`, `$FF` → current `$6140` |
| `ObjInit` `30:C25D` | 11/11 | all 32 `$0300-$06E0` columns of the slot vs `objtype_dump.decode(type)` |
| `AreaLoad` `31:E14A` | 2/2 | `$07E6/$07E7`, R4/R5 `$07F4/$07F5`, and per spawned slot x/y (handler-1 `y\|=$0E`), `$0540`, `$0560` vs `area_dump.decode` |
| `SndStartMusic` `24:80E4` | 1/1 | `$F5/$F6` = `SndMusicTable[id]`, channel 0-3 ptrs = header +1/+3/+5/+7, wait = len = 1, octave 0 |
| `SaveGame` `23:BC09` | 2/2 × 9 | the image against its source RAM, field by field (table "Current save image"), incl. `+201-2FF` = 0 and the `$6D00` mirror |

End-of-run RAM checks (area 1): k0 `$62FC-$62FF` + grid + music `$0102`, k1 `$07E0-$07E3`, `$69`, `$07F0/$07F1`, and `$67&$FC` all match. (The low 2 bits of `$67` hold the last quadrant page, see `MetatileExpand`.) Every active `obj_active` id has a non-null `ObjAiTable` entry. Active sound channel ptrs are in `$8000-$BFFF` and at or past their stream starts. `title.state` confirms the **boot copy quirk**: at the title `$6480` page == `$7D00` page and `$6580` page == `$7E00` page.

New facts from the run:
- **Two saves before control:** `SaveGame` runs at f2348 (new game: area 0, `0088,0078`) and at f2433, 24 frames after entering area 1 (area 1, `00B0,0056`, = k2 entry 0). Area 1 is an autosave area.
- **Frame order:** `ObjSpriteDraw` runs **before** `ObjCollideAll` in every gameplay frame (61 frames `[draw coll]`; 4 cut-scene frames draw only). So collision uses the screen positions `$05C0/$05E0` written earlier in the same frame.
- **Slot 0** follows the player: at f2460 it sits at the player's x/y with AI `$03` (`27:9D9A`) and meta id `$A7`. Slot 3 (AI `$05`, `27:A219`, meta `$4C` = empty) sits 4 px lower.
- **k2 = player start positions, not a camera**; `$34-$37` is a position cursor (see the area sub-table row k2 and the RAM Map).

#### Reference cross-check (session 40, `tools/ref/` → `docs/ref_compare.txt`)

**Update (session 43):** the capture now exists (12 frames in `ref/`, made by hand after session 40), and the pixel compare has run. See the "Reference pixels (FCEUX)" row of the "Verification Log" and `COMPARISON.md` §1. The paragraph below describes the state in session 40.

**Status: no reference capture exists yet**, so every emulator result in this file (sessions 36-39, `verify.py`, `--compare`, `render_compare.py`) is still **self-emu only**. That is especially true for PPU/IRQ timing (HUD split line 190/192, the Y-scroll `$EC` 4-px offset, MMC3 A12 clocking). FCEUX is installed (`/usr/games/fceux`), but this session needed approval to launch it, so the capture was not made. To make it from the repo root: delete `rom/re-c.sav` if it exists, run `fceux --loadlua tools/ref/fceux_capture.lua rom/re-c.nes` (add `xvfb-run -a` in front when there is no display), then run `python3 tools/ref/compare_ref.py`.
- **`tools/ref/fceux_capture.lua`**: powers on, replays the canonical `--keys` script (`joypad.set(1, …)` before each `frameadvance`), and after frames 120 600 1200 1450 1500 2250 2352 2385 2392 2409 2450 2460 writes `ref/frame_NNNN.{ram,wram,nt,pal,png}` plus `ref/capture.txt`. The files are RAM `$0000-$07FF`, WRAM `$6000-$7FFF`, PPU `$2000-$2FFF` and `$3F00-$3F1F` (via `ppu.readbyte`), and a screenshot from `gui.savescreenshotas`. It detects which API is present: `emu`/`FCEU`, `memory.readbyterange` or a byte loop, and a `gui.gdscreenshot` `.gd` fallback. The packaged doc (`/usr/share/doc/fceux/UsingLuaScripting-ListofFunctions.txt.gz`, read with the new `tools/gzgrep.py`) only lists the old `FCEU.*` API. FCEUX Lua cannot read OAM, so OAM is compared through the `$0200` shadow page, which `31:F3D3` DMAs every NMI. **The script itself has not been run yet.**
- **`tools/ref/compare_ref.py`**: runs `tools.emu` from power-on with the same keys and snapshots each needed frame. It matches each reference frame N with emu frame N+d, choosing d in ±3 (`--search`) to give the fewest RAM diffs. The offset is needed because FCEUX frames start at vblank while `tools.emu` frames end at the 261→0 wrap. It then reports RAM byte diffs with labels (OAM page excluded), OAM sprite diffs (64 × 4 bytes), WRAM diff ranges, nametable diffs per 1KB table, palette diffs (mirrors folded, values masked with `$3F`), and a **palette-independent pixel diff**. For the pixel diff, each emu colour is mapped to the reference colour it most often lies on, so FCEUX's RGB palette does not need to match `NES_RGB`; per-tile-row counts are listed. Exit 0 = identical, 1 = diffs, 2 = no capture (the case today).
- **Self-test of the comparator** (`--make-fake DIR [--shift 1] [--tamper]` writes `tools.emu` captures in the reference format with inverted RGB and labels shifted by −1): the clean fake gives **5/5 frames identical, offset +1 found every time**, with colour maps 2/4/12/13/11 → same counts. `--tamper` (`$0040` ^`$55`, OAM byte `$0214`, `$3F01`, 8×8 block at 64,64) is reported exactly: 1 RAM diff `$0040 main_state`, OAM sprite 5, `$3F01`, 64 px in tile row 8; exit 1. The comparator works, and it is ready for a real capture.

#### Reference-compare triage (session 73)

Items of the "Ph6 reference-compare triage" task. New tools: `tools/ref/fill_probe.py` (→ `docs/ref_fill_probe.txt`), `tools/emu/hud_split_probe.py`, `tools/emu/sample_point_probe.py`; `NES(ram_fill=, wram_fill=)` (int, `'zero'`, `'ff'`, `'fceux'` or bytes; `tools/emu/nes.py` `RAM_FILLS`).
- **(d) bank shadow at f2460: sample point, benign.** `sample_point_probe` from `area1_enter.state` (f2450-2463): at line 241 dot 0 (vblank start, where FCEUX's Lua frame callback samples) tools.emu has `$6E/$6F` = `1A/1B` and `$09` = `00` every frame, the same as FCEUX. At the 261→0 wrap (tools.emu's frame end) it has `00/01` and `$09` = `01`. The main thread is still in bank pair 13 (26/27 object code) when vblank starts, and the NMI/frame end comes later. No mapper difference.
- **(e) OAM sprites 59/61/63: power-on fill, benign.** These are hidden sprites (y = `$F0`). `ObjHideUnusedOam` only writes their Y byte, so tile/attr/x (`$02ED-$02EF`, `$02F5-$02F7`, `$02FD-$02FF`) keep their power-on value: FCEUX `FF FF FF`, tools.emu `00 00 00`. All three ranges have address bit 2 set, so FCEUX's `00×4 FF×4` fill gives `$FF` there. The even sprites in between have bit 2 clear (`$00` in both), so they agree. The 22-28 sprite diffs in gameplay frames 2352-2409 are moving objects at a ±3 frame offset (the f2463 sample shows 50 sprites differing against the f2460 ref; it is just a different frame).
- **(g) screen line 191: mid-line split phase, benign.** `hud_split_probe` (f2450/2451): the IRQ enters `IrqHandler1Hud` at line 190 dot 38/39. After `DelayX`, PPUMASK is written at line 190 dot 338 (hblank), then the 2nd PPUADDR write (`$2B00`) at **line 191 dot 45** (x 44), PPUSCROLL at x 62, R0=`$3C` at x 98 and R1=`$38` at x 134. So on line 191 the PPU fetches HUD nametable tiles through the playfield CHR banks until R0/R1 switch: a garbage stripe that hardware shows too. Against `ref/frame_2450.png`, lines 190 and 192 are identical and line 191 differs in 27 px (x 56-119). The FCEUX line is **the tools.emu line shifted right by exactly 8 px** (new tiles start at x 64 in FCEUX, x 56 in tools.emu), while the CHR-switch positions agree. So FCEUX's `$2006` reload reaches the tile fetch one 8-dot fetch later. That is within the not-modelled 2-3 dot `$2006` delay plus FCEUX's tile-granular old PPU. Neither emulator is cycle-exact here, so it is not a game-logic difference and nothing in tools.emu needs changing. The web port should match tools.emu.
- **(b) uninitialised RAM: FCEUX fill confirmed (session 74, from `docs/ref_fill_probe.txt` of s73).** Of the bytes that tools.emu never writes (they differ between the `$00` and `$A5` fill runs), 97-99 % of the title-frame bytes and 85-88 % of the gameplay-frame bytes equal `RAM_FILLS['fceux']` (`00×4 FF×4`). `zero`/`ff` hit only ~50 %. FCEUX WRAM is all zero at power-on (6588/6588 at f120). The rest of the "unwritten" bytes are not left at their fill value: the game copies or derives them from uninitialised RAM (`$0045`, `$00F0-$00FD`, `$0104-$0107`, `$014A-$017E`, stack). With `NES(ram_fill='fceux', wram_fill='zero')` the remaining RAM diffs per ref frame are 5-41 (title) and 17-35 (gameplay), and every one is explained below or in (c)/(d). So `compare_ref`-style compares should use the fceux fill, and stack page `$0100-$01FF` diffs (return addresses/temps at a different sample point) are benign.
- **(c) frame offset: three phase regimes, not a steady drift (session 74, `tools/ref/drift_probe.py` → `docs/ref_drift.txt`, `tools/emu/lag_probe.py`).** compare_ref's ±3 "fewest diffs" offset is noisy on static screens, so drift_probe scans ±12 and pins the offset with per-pass counters. `title_ctr` `$06FF` (DEC once per `MainState3` pass, first written at **tools.emu f3**) matches at exactly one d: **f120 d=−4 (0 RAM+WRAM diffs), f600-f2250 d=−3** (f1200: 0 diffs). So tools.emu's boot reaches the bank-18 intro 4 frames earlier than FCEUX's `emu.poweron()` + `frameadvance` (FCEUX power-on/PPU warm-up timing; not checkable without an FCEUX per-frame trace), and one lag frame between f120 and f600 brings that to 3. `lag_probe` lists **17 tools.emu lag frames** in f214-2320 (frames with no `$06FF` write): 858, 1239, 1422-1423, 1602, 1604, 1702-1703 (screen set-ups with rendering off), **1807-1808, 1951-1952, 2063-2064, 2175-2176** (status step 3 `StatusTypeStep` `19:A34E`: at each text-line end `PpuQueueDrainWait` `19:A092` spins until the NMI has drained the queue, which costs 2 frames), and 2320. They show up as the 10-frame gaps in `status_anim_ctr` `$0617` (it steps every 8 passes, `$06FF & 7`, in `StatusAnimTick` `19:A212`). Input-driven events resync both emulators to the key frame: the START at 1700 starts `title_mode` 4 at f1703 in tools.emu, and the next autonomous event, name entry (`title_step` 5, tools.emu f2248), comes **~6 frames earlier in FCEUX**. `name_entry_frames` `$6423` (INC per `NameEntryInput` pass) is `$08` in FCEUX at f2250 (tools.emu reaches that at f2255, d=+5) and ends at **`$3A` (FCEUX) vs `$35` (tools.emu)** when the START at 2300 freezes it. FCEUX has the same net lag count over f1500-2250 (`$06FF` d stays −3), so its lag frames fall at other points of the f1600-2248 chain. The 2-frame queue-drain lags are the likely place (they depend on where in the frame the main thread reaches `19:A092`, i.e. CPU cycles vs NMI). **Gameplay:** after the START at 2300 (fade-out f2301-2318, `main_state` 3→0 f2319, 1 f2321), d is **+1** at every gameplay ref frame (f2352-2460; `tmp_08` and 17-22 residual diffs), so tools.emu's fade-out/area-load chain is 1 frame longer. The per-frame `--compare` CSV can't find these points (FCEUX has 12 samples only), so `tools/ref/fceux_capture.lua` now also writes **`ref/trace.txt`** (one line per frame: `$40 $41 $06F0 $06F1 $06F3 $06FF $0617 $0008 $6423`), and `lag_probe.py --trace FILE` writes the same columns from tools.emu. Rerun FCEUX (needs approval) and diff the two files to place each lag frame exactly. **Verdict: benign for game logic.** Every offset comes from frame timing (boot length, the queue-drain spin), not from a different code path.
- **(f) WRAM `$6423/$6D23/$7D23/$70F4-$70F5`: one root cause, timing (session 74).** `$6423` = `name_entry_frames` (above): `$3A` vs `$35`. `SaveGame` copies the `$6400` page to `$6D00` and `$7D00`, so `$6D23`/`$7D23` follow, and `save_checksum` `$70F5` (+ its copy `$70F4`) sums `$7D00-$7FFF`: FCEUX `7F 7F`, tools.emu `7A 7A` = exactly the `$3A`−`$35` = 5 difference. Likewise RAM `$0628-$062B` (`title_blink_timer`, FCEUX = emu+1 from f2352 on) is a per-slot name-entry countdown (`19:A1EC`) that froze at the START. In tools.emu `19:A1EC` runs once more at **f2320**, the frame after `main_state` → 0, with no `$06FF` write that frame (so it is not a `MainState3` pass; which caller runs it is still open), so its timers are 1 lower. FCEUX does not get that extra decrement. `$060C/$0614/$0617/$0627/$06FF` diffs in gameplay frames are the same frozen title counters. **Benign.** The `$6009-$601F`, `$6120-$612F` (attr shadow) and `$6204` diffs at f2409 belong to (h) (blank/fade frame, area 0→1 load).
- **(h) ref f2409 "blank vs faded room": a comparator artifact, both emulators are blank (session 84, `tools/ref/blank_probe.py`).** The s43 claim came from `pixel_diff`'s per-colour vote map: a 1-colour reference maps every tools.emu colour onto that colour, so any picture scores 0 px, and the old ±3 *pixel* offset search picked d=−3, a faded-room frame. Pixel-comparing ref f2409 against tools.emu f2404-f2414: f2404-f2405 show the faded room (7 colours), f2406-f2408 the darker fade step (4 colours), and **tools.emu is blank (1 colour) from f2409 on**. With the gameplay offset d=+1 (drift_probe) or the RAM-best d=+2 (`compare_ref`), ref f2409 = tools.emu f2410/f2411, both blank, so **the blank frame matches**. FCEUX's *first* blank frame can't be placed from one screenshot; it is ≤ f2409, and tools.emu's first blank is f2409 = ref f2408 at d=+1, which fits. The WRAM diffs there are working buffers of the area-load stream: `$6009-$601F` = the `BgRowRender` tile buffer `$6000`, `$6120-$612F` = the attr shadow `$6100`, `$6204` = a PPU queue ring record `$6200`. They change on every frame of the load (tools.emu `$6009` = `00 12 13 3E 12 11` over f2409-2414), and the ref values appear in tools.emu one or two frames later (`$6120`=`5A` at f2411, `$6204`=`23` at f2410). **Benign** (sample point inside the load). **`pixel_diff` now has a fold flag** (`fold_check`, `compare_ref.py`): a ref colour that absorbs ≥ 3 tools.emu colours, each covering ≥ 0.5 % of the screen, is reported as `FOLD` and makes the frame non-identical. `blank_probe` flags f2404-f2408 against ref f2409 and nothing at f2409+. In `docs/ref_compare.txt` (rerun s84; the committed s41 copy was still pre-crop) no frame folds. Self-test `compare_ref.py --screens --tamper`: each §4 screen against itself is 0 px, against another screen it differs or folds (equip vs the near-blank gameover screen: `FOLD`, which the old map would have scored as 479 px only).
- **(m) FCEUX OSD in the `.gd` screenshots (session 101).** `gui.gdscreenshot()` includes FCEUX's message overlay: a translucent box with white text at the bottom left ("Power on" in f120, "Snapshot Saved." from the previous frame's capture in f1500 and f2352-2460). `compare_ref.osd_box()` finds it as ≥ 64 px in the bottom 40 lines whose colours occur nowhere above them, with a bounding box < x128 and ≤ 16 lines high, and masks that box + 2 px from both the colour vote and the bad-pixel count (reported as `FCEUX OSD masked xA-B yC-D`). A real game difference in that area would still show if it uses picture colours, or if the region is larger/wider than an OSD box. Result: all tile-row-26/27 diffs gone; only the (g) line-191 stripe (17-37 px, f2352-2460) is left. Suppressing the OSD in FCEUX itself (config `SubtitleDisplay`/Lua) was not needed and FCEUX was not relaunched.
- **(i) menu/death/save steps in the FCEUX script (session 84).** `tools/ref/fceux_capture.lua` now saves a savestate after frame 2450 (= tools.emu `area1_enter.state`) and replays the 8 `COMPARISON.md` §4 scenarios from it (`SCREENS` table, same `p:`/`w:`/`s:` steps as `tools/emu/probe_screens.py`), writing `ref/screen_NAME.{ram,wram,nt,pal,png}`. `python3 tools/ref/compare_ref.py --screens` pixel-diffs them against `gfx/compare/screens/NAME.png` (exit 2 until FCEUX has been rerun). Not run yet: launching FCEUX needs user approval. FCEUX is installed at `/usr/games/fceux`; no `luac` is installed, so the script's syntax has not been machine-checked. *(s91: the run happened by hand after s84 and the script ran clean; the screenshots are stale, see (l).)*
- **(k) lag frames placed with the FCEUX per-frame trace (session 91, `tools/ref/trace_diff.py` → `docs/ref_trace_diff.txt`, `tools/emu/caller_probe.py`).** The `ref/trace.txt` columns (`$40 $41 $06F0 $06F1 $06F3 $06FF $0617 $0008 $6423`) vs `lag_probe --trace docs/emu_trace.txt`. "No `$06FF` write" frames: FCEUX 1-6, 216, 861, 1242, 1421-1422, 1602, 1604, 1702-1703, 1802-1803, 1946-1947, 2058-2059, 2170-2171, 2319+; tools.emu 1-2, 212-213, 858, 1239, 1422-1423, 1602, 1604, 1702-1703, 1807-1808, 1951-1952, 2063-2064, 2175-2176, 2320+. Placement:
  - **Boot, d=−4.** FCEUX keeps `$40`=0 for f0-3 (`$40`=2 at f4, `$06F3`=1 at f6, first `$06FF` write f7). tools.emu has `$40`=2 already at its f0 sample and writes `$06FF` first at f3. So the 4 frames are before the first `MainState2Init` pass: FCEUX's `poweron()` + reset/PPU warm-up takes 4 frames longer. This is emulator power-on timing, not game code.
  - **Intro → title (f212-217), −4 → −3.** tools.emu has 2 no-write frames (f212-213, `$06F3`=0 then `$0617`=0, `$40`=3 at f214); FCEUX has 1 (f216, `$40`=3 at f217). One FCEUX-only frame is gone here, *not* between f120 and f600 as s74 guessed. f217-f1420 then run at d=−3 (f858/861 and f1239/1242 = the same screen set-up lag 3 frames apart).
  - **Input-locked events, no offset.** Lags after the START presses (f1421-1422 vs f1422-1423, f1602/f1604 both, f1702-1703 both) fall on (almost) the same absolute frames, because the key script is in absolute frames. The `$06F0` 2→3→4 changes after the f1700 START are at f1702/f1705 in both.
  - **Status chain (f1705-f2248), 5 frames later in tools.emu.** `status_anim_ctr` `$0617` (=`$0D` at f1705 in both) is decremented by `StatusAnimTick` `19:A212` only when `title_ctr & 7` = 0 (`19:A251`). Under the 3-frame counter offset, FCEUX hits `$06FF`=`$38` at f1705 and tools.emu hits `$30` at f1710, so every gated step (the 2-frame text-line lags `PpuQueueDrainWait` and the start of name entry) comes 5 frames later in tools.emu: 1802/1807, 1946/1951, 2058/2063, 2170/2175. This is the s74 "FCEUX reaches name entry ~6 frames sooner": the phase of an 8-frame gate, not a CPU/PPU timing difference. `name_entry_frames` `$6423` ends at `$3A` (FCEUX) vs `$36` (tools.emu).
  - **f2300 START, +1.** FCEUX sets `title_step` 6 (`$06F1`, with `$06F3`=2) at f2300 and tools.emu at f2301. Then both run 17 fade frames + 2 `$06F3`=0 passes (19 `MainState3` passes from the f2300 row: `$06FF` ED→DB vs EA→D7) and `main_state` 0 is seen at f2319 in both. `main_state` 1 comes at f2320 vs f2321, and every later `$41` change keeps d=+1 (8 at f2349/2350, 1 at 2390/2391, 8 at 2434/2435). So **the whole gameplay offset +1 comes from this START being read one frame later**, not from a lag frame in the f2300-2350 fade/load. Most likely cause (not traced): name entry reads the pad in a pass that starts in vblank (the NMI → main-loop pass begins before tools.emu's frame boundary at the 261→0 wrap), so the tools.emu key for frame N reaches it one pass later. At f1700 the pass reads the pad later in the frame, so both agree there.
  - **`19:A1EC` "at f2320" (s74) was a misread.** `caller_probe` hooks show it only at f2319: 4 entries (one per slot) from `TitleStatusScreen` `JSR 19:A17C` → `StatusSlotAnim` `19:A1AA` → `StatusSlotCountdown` `19:A1EC`, in the last regular `MainState3` pass (`$06FF` D8→D7 written, `main_state` still 3 at the call and set to 0 later in the pass). It has no other caller. The frozen `$0628-$062B` (FCEUX = emu+1) is therefore a phase residue of the 5-frame status-chain shift, not an extra decrement.
- **(l) FCEUX screens compared by state (session 91, `tools/ref/screen_state_compare.py` → `docs/ref_screens.txt`, `gfx/compare/ref_screens.json`).** `compare_ref.py --screens` reported 1/8 pixel matches (66-75 % differing), but every `ref/screen_*.png` is the same file as `ref/frame_2450.png` (md5 `40447687…`; `frame_2460.png` too, and `frame_1450.png` = `frame_1500.png`): `speedmode("maximum")` skips rendering, so `gui.savescreenshotas` saves an old picture. (The gameover PNG differs only by chance. Pixel results for `frame_2460` in §1 are therefore against the f2450 picture too.) The RAM/NT/PAL dumps are live. Compared by state from a fill=`fceux` canonical run to f2451, **all 8 screens have identical nametables, palette and OAM shadow**; the leftover RAM/WRAM bytes are listed in the Verification Log row "Reference screens §4". `fceux_capture.lua` now runs at `speedmode("normal")`, so a future rerun gives real PNGs.

#### Object type record (`ObjTypeTable` `13:AC00`, read by `ObjInit` `30:C25D`)

Index = type (`$11`); type ≥ `$80` uses the upper half `13:AD00`. Type 1 = no-op. The record is a sparse initialiser for the slot columns:

| Offset | Field | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | `snd` | ≠0 → `PlaySound(snd)`. Then all 32 `$0300-$06E0` columns of the slot are zeroed. Type `$FF` stops here |
| +1 | `m0` | bitmask, MSB first: each set bit consumes the next byte → `$0300`,`$0320`,…,`$03E0` |
| … | `m1` + bytes | → `$0400`…`$04E0` |
| … | `m2` + bytes | → `$0500`…`$05E0` |
| … | `m3` + bytes | → `$0600`…`$06E0` (`$06E0` = bit 0) |

What each column means → "Object slot struct". Init counts per column → `docs/obj_struct_inits.txt`. Position, `$0540`, `$0560` and (handler 1) `$0300/$0320/$0680/$06A0/$06E0` are written by the spawn handler after `ObjInit`.

#### Area record + header sub-tables

**Area record** (`AreaPtrTable` `12:9201`[`$6C`], body read by `LoadAreaChrRecord` `31:E16D`; ptr hi b7 clear = no record):

| Offset | Field | Dest / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | `00` | must be 0, else the record is ignored |
| +1, +2 | `e6`, `e7` | `$07E6/$07E7` sprite palette ids 2/3 (flag items override with `($6C&7)+9`) |
| +3, +4 | `R4`, `R5` | `$07F4/$07F5` sprite CHR 1KB banks (`$FF` = bank 127) |
| +5 … | spawn entries `b0 b1 b2 b3` | one per slot from `$0D`; `FF` = end |

Spawn entry: `b0` = y cell (Y = b0·16+12; `FE` = skip slot); `b1` = x cell (b7 = skip slot; X = (b1&`$7F`)·16+8); `b2` b0-2 = handler (`SpawnHandlerTable` `31:E218`, 0-4), b0-1 → `$0540`, b6 = x+8, b7 → `$0380` b5 (tile+`$40`); `b3` = param → `$0560`. **Handler-1 record** (banks 4/5, `$8000+(p>>2)·$100+$F0+(p&3)·4`): `$20-$23` = `FF type` or `$0680 $06A0 behav/tile meta`.

**Header** (`10:8300`[`$6C`], `AreaSubTable` `30:C7A5`(k)) = k0-k4 ptr16 (k5 only in areas `6A 6D 6E 8A 9F A2 A5 AE AF B4`):

| k | Record | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | map (once) | `snd W H f62FE f62FF` + grid (H+1 rows × W+1 ids, row stride 8 → `$6300`; `$80` = black page) |
| 1 | gfx (once) | `p0 p1 p2` → `$07E0-$07E2` (`$07E3`=`$7F`), `v67` tileset, `v69`, `R0 R1` → `$07F0/$07F1` |
| 2 | entry points | 4 B `xlo xhi ylo yhi` = **player start position** → `$34-$37` (`pos_*`), index `$6D`&`$1F` (corrected s39, was "camera") |
| 3 | exits | 4 B `xcell ycell area entry`, `FF` end (`ExitLookup` `31:EB09`) |
| 4 | persistent screens | 2 B `flag scr` (scr = y_hi<<4\|x_hi), `FF` end → `$64C0` bits |
| 5 | edge links | 4 B `area a21 a22 a23`, no terminator (`31:E573`) |

#### Screen page + metatile tables

| Table | Location | Layout |
|---|---|---|
| Screen page | screen id S → bank 2·(S>>6)(+1) page S&`$3F` (`$62FF` b7 and S&`$3F`<`$20` → bank 10 page S&`$1F`, row path only) | 240 B = 16×15 metatile ids, byte (y&`$F0`)\|(x>>4); tail `$F0-$FF` reused (palette / spawn records) |
| Metatile set | banks 8/9, `$67&$FC` + 0..3 | 4 parallel 256-byte pages = TL, TR, BL, BR tile of metatile M |
| Metatile attrs | `9:B000 + i·$40`, i = (`$67`&`$3C`)>>2 via `MetatileAttrPtrTable` `31:EEEF` | 64 B, 2-bit BG palette per metatile, 4 per byte, LSB first |
| Visited substitution | `9:BE00 + ($67&$3C)·8` | 32 B: replacement for metatile M < `$20` on visited screens |

#### Metasprite record (banks 28/29, `ObjDrawMeta` `28:822E`)

Id = `$0300` + (`$0380` b0 ? 0 : `$0360`); id < `$80` → `ObjMetaPtrTableA` `28:845C`, else `ObjMetaPtrTableB` `28:855C`.

| Form | Bytes | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| redirect | `FF lo hi` | draw the record at `hi:lo` horizontally mirrored (x' = −dx−8, attr ^ `$40`) |
| empty | `00` | nothing drawn |
| frames | `N M` + frames | N sprites per frame, frame = (`$04E0`>>3)&M at +2+f·N·4 |
| sprite | `dx dy attr tile` | dx/dy s8; dx=`$80` ends the frame; attr b2 = "lower part" (stop here when `$0380` b6) |

#### Palette record (banks 2/3, `PaletteLoadOne` `26:8C6E`)

Slot s (BG id p, or sprite id p+`$B0` mod 256) → bank 2+(s>>7), `$A000 + ((s>>2)&$1F)·$100 + $F0 + (s&3)·4`. Record = 4 NES colours; `$FF` = copy of the backdrop `$6140` (s53: the value `pal_buf[0]` holds *at that moment*, already faded, and then the fade is subtracted again. If p0's own colour 0 is `$FF`, it copies the previous area's backdrop, or `$FF` → `$0F` after Reset). Built into `pal_buf` `$6140-$615F` (minus fade `$11`; negative → `$0F`), uploaded by `PaletteUpload` `31:F8CB`.

#### Sound headers + channel state (bank 24/25, SFX data bank 12)

| Struct | Layout |
|---|---|
| Music header (`SndMusicTable` `24:8C0C`[id], id `$00-$1F`) | `01` (unused) + 4 stream ptr16 (SQ1, SQ2, TRI, NOISE) |
| SFX header (`12:A000`[id−`$20`], id `$20-$77`) | priority (lower wins; rejected if > `$0112`) + 2 stream ptr16 (SQ2, NOISE), stored as `$8xxx` (+`$20` hi at load) |
| Stream | op bytes, see the *Stream bytes* table in "Sound data format" |

Channel state = 6-wide arrays indexed X (0 SQ1, 1 SQ2, 2 TRI, 3 NOISE, 4 SFX-SQ2, 5 SFX-NOISE):

| Base | Label | Field |
|---|---|---|
| `$0114` | `snd_ch_flags` | b0 active, b4 rest/hold, b5 vibrato, b6 software envelope, b7 stolen by SFX |
| `$011A/$0120` | `snd_ch_ptr_lo/hi` | stream pointer |
| `$0126/$0132`, `$013E` | –, `snd_ch_loop1_cnt` | loop1 return ptr lo/hi, count |
| `$012C/$0138`, `$0144` | – | loop2 / `A0` call return ptr lo/hi, count |
| `$014A` | `snd_ch_wait` | frames left until the next op read |
| `$0150` | `snd_ch_note_len` | note length (0 = 256) |
| `$0156` | `snd_ch_octave` | octave·16 |
| `$015C` | `snd_ch_env` | envelope# <<4 \| step |
| `$0162` | `snd_ch_vib` | vibrato depth<<3 \| phase |
| `$0168` | `snd_ch_reg0` | `$4000`-style reg shadow (duty/vol) |
| `$016E` | `snd_ch_sweep` | sweep reg shadow |
| `$0174/$017A` | `snd_ch_period_lo/hi` | base period |

Globals: `snd_flags` `$F0`, `snd_stream_ofs` `$F1`, `snd_frame_end` `$F2`, `snd_op` `$F3`, `snd_ptr` `$F5/$F6`, `snd_tmp` `$F7`, `snd_chn_shadow` `$FB`, `snd_reg_dirty` `$FC`, `snd_op_count` `$FD`, `snd_fade_timer` `$FE`, `snd_dmc_drum` `$FF`; queue/API `$0100-$0113` (RAM Map). The zp and channel labels were renamed from the session-29 auto names (`snd_zpF1`…, `snd_ch0114`…) in session 33.

#### RAM Map (session 29, `tools/ram_map.py` → `docs/ram_map.txt`)

`ram_map.py` re-decodes every code line of `disasm/bank_NN.asm` (`.db` rows skipped) and counts each reference to `$0000-$07FF` / `$6000-$7FFF`: reads, writes (INC/DEC/shift = both), index register (`X`, `Y`, `(),Y`, `(,X)`; a `(zp),Y` counts as a read of both pointer bytes), per-bank r/w, and the labels.csv name (unlabelled addresses inside a labelled array show `(base+d)`). It also finds **parallel arrays** (indexed bases at a fixed stride, same index register, chains of ≥3). `--csv out.csv` writes the table; `--emit-labels N` / `--append-labels N` name every unlabelled address with ≥N r+w refs. Static RE only — the access counts are per code site, not per execution.

Result: **612 distinct addresses** referenced; 118 were labelled, now **364** (246 labels added this session, all with ≥3 refs; generated names keep an `auto (ram_map.py s29)` comment with the r/w counts and banks so later sessions can rename them).

|---|---|---|---|---|

| `$0A-$31` | `tmp_0A-tmp_31` (+ `jmp_trampoline` `$10`) | 40 | zp scratch | Shared by every bank; meanings are per routine (`$11`/`$19` fade, `$12/$13` hit-flag params, `$20-$2F` text/menu param block, `$20` BG-probe attr bits). `SaveGame` saves `$10-$2F` to `$6000`; `FadeSaveZpToWram` saves `$0A-$29` |

#### Object slot struct (session 30, `tools/struct_dump.py obj` → `docs/obj_struct.txt`)

*Static RE only.* Per column: code-site reads/writes, fixed-slot refs, writer/reader routines and how many `ObjTypeTable` records initialise it are in `docs/obj_struct.txt` (`--sites` lists every site); `struct_dump.py objtype` → `docs/obj_struct_inits.txt` gives the per-column value histogram over all type records.

**Object-vs-object collision (session 30)** — `ObjCollideAll` `26:8F6E` (called once per frame from `SubstPlay` `30:CB7E`):
- Clears `$0541` and `$0643`. Even frames run pair records 7…0, odd frames 14…8 (`$08` b0), so each record runs every other frame.
- `CollPairTable` `26:96F1`, 15 × 4 bytes `slot first last handler`: **attacker slot** vs **target slots first+1 … last−1** (all records use `0C 1F` → targets `$0D-$1E`), handler = `HitHandlerTable` `26:8FBB` index·2.

  | attacker slot(s) | handler | meaning |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 (player) | 2 → `26:9357` | enemy touches player (records 6 and 13 → every frame) |
  | 3 | 4 → `26:94B0` | records 7 and 14 → every frame |
  | `$0C` | 6 → `HitHandler3` `26:8FC4` | records 5 and 11 → every frame |
  | 2, 4-11 | 0 → `26:90AA` | player shots/sword (once every other frame each); hit handler 0 → `EnemyKilled` |
- `ObjOverlapScan` `26:95F8` (X = attacker, scans Y from `$10`+1 to `$11`−1, C=1 = hit, Y = target): skipped if `obj_flags` b7 or `obj_flags2` b7 is set on either object, or if the attacker's `$03A0` high nibble is 0. A target qualifies when `(its $03A0 & attacker $03A0) & $F0` ≠ 0, so the **high nibble of `$03A0` is a collision-group mask**. Box = `HitboxTable` `26:9691`[(`$03A0`&`$0F`)·4 \| (`obj_hitflags` & `$40`)] = signed `dx w dy h` added to the screen position `$05C0/$05E0` (so `$0420` b6 selects hitboxes 16-23). It is a plain AABB overlap test: target.x0 < attacker.x1, target.x1 ≥ attacker.x0, same for y.
- `HitboxTable` has 24 entries (`26:9691-96F0`; `struct_dump.py rec hitbox 26:9691 24`), so with b6 set only lo nibbles 0-7 are valid. Examples: 0 = `-6 12 -21 20` (player-sized), 5 = `-13 26 -29 18`, 8 = `-8 32 -32 48`, 11 = `-22 44 -30 44` (large boss), 18/19 = `0 40 -24 40` / `-40 40 -24 40` (right/left-only boxes), 21 = all zero (never hits), 22/23 = `-3 6 -30 60` / `-30 60 -3 6` (thin vertical/horizontal beams).
- `26:95C0` (helper used by the hit handlers): sets the target's `obj_flags2` b7 (keeping the low nibble) and copies the attacker's direction into the target's `obj_meta_dir` high nibble (`$0340` ≥ `$0B` halves it first). This is the knockback direction.
- So **`$03A0` = `obj_coll`** (hi = group mask, lo = hitbox id). 189 type records initialise it.

**Per-frame order inside `ObjUpdateAll` `30:CCCC` for slot X (session 30):**
1. `obj_flags2` hi nibble ≠ 0 → **knockback**: `$0340` += `$10` each frame (set to `$8x` by `HitKnockbackSet`, so 8 frames until it wraps to 0). For slots ≠ 1 with `obj_active` b7 clear: move in direction `obj_meta_dir`>>4 at speed 8 via `ObjMoveSlide` (`$0340` temporarily = 8), `DEC obj_timer`, restore, **skip the AI**. The player (slot 1) falls through to its normal AI (it handles its own knockback). When the counter wraps, `obj_meta_dir` hi nibble is cleared.
2. `obj_freeze` `$05A0` ≠ 0 → DEC; still ≠ 0 → **skip the AI**. Reaching 0 on an object with `obj_spawn_b2`=1 → `HitSetFlag` clear (`$12`=0, `$13`=`obj_spawn_param`).
3. AI dispatch via `ObjAiTable` (above).

*(s80 details, fuzz-checked):* the loop clears `obj_active` of slot `$1F` on entry (`30:CCCE` `STX $04BF`, X=0), so slot 31 never runs an AI. It re-runs `SwitchBank16($0D)` before **every** slot, so an AI that remaps R6/R7 (bank-15 bosses, `ObjBgProbe` leaving R7 = `$1B`) cannot affect the next slot. The knockback slide maps bank 26 by `SetR6($1A)` and leaves R7 = 27. When the counter wraps (or the object has `obj_active` b7), the hi nibble of `obj_meta_dir` is cleared and **the AI still runs that frame**. The `$05A0`-reaches-0 hit-flag clear runs before the dispatch of the same frame. `JSR $0010` writes `$10-$12` = `4C lo hi` even for null entries (the null test comes after the stores).

**Web port (s80, Ph7 web 5/8 (a)):** `web/src/game/obj/objects.js` `objUpdateAll` (a generator, because AI `$1A`'s sequence `30:CD8A` waits on the PPU queue and on frames; that path is still logged as unported) + `hitSetFlag` `26:9011`, `AI_TABLE` (id → handler; unported ids log `AI $xx not ported` once and do nothing). `web/src/game/obj/move.js`: `objMoveSlide` `26:98D7`, `objMoveStep` `26:9947`, `objScreenEdge` `26:99FF`. Checked by `obj_check.mjs` against `web/test/obj_frames/fuzz_update.json`: `ObjMoveSlide` 200/200, `ObjUpdateAll` 174/174 identical. The other 26 cases spawn an object (shot / death type) whose AI is not ported and run it later in the same pass; the check counts those as gaps, not failures. A negative test (drop the knockback `DEC obj_timer`) fails 173/174.

#### Save data + WRAM layout (session 31, `tools/save_dump.py` → `docs/save_layout.txt`)

*Static RE only.* `save_dump.py` reads the copy descriptors, the boot page tables, the slot descriptors and the new-save template straight from the ROM. It prints the copy lengths with the `BlockCopy` quirk next to the intended lengths, plus a byte-ownership map of the current save image. The save/load routines themselves are described under "Save / load module" (session 11); this section covers the boot side and the field layout.

**Boot save check** (`BootSaveCheck` `31:F0A4`, every boot, called from `WarmBootContinue` `31:F39F`; was `BootSub`). It sets `$7001` (`save_reset_flag`) = 0, saves R7 in `$7005`, maps bank 23 at `$A000` (for the template), then checks three images in turn:
1. **Slot 0** (`$7100-$73FF` primary, `$7400-$76FF` backup):
   - Primary == backup (all 768 bytes) → only check the `AMI` tag at `$71FD`. A bad tag → `SaveSlot0Reset` `31:F11F`.
   - Otherwise `SaveSumSlot`(0) sums the primary into `$16` and compares the result with `save_csum0_a` `$70F0`. On a match, `SlotCopy`(0) copies primary→backup and both checksums are set to the sum.
   - Otherwise the backup is tried the same way against `$70F1`. On a match it is restored to the primary with `SlotCopy`(1). If both fail → `SaveSlot0Reset`.
2. **Slot 1** (`$7700` / `$7A00`, checksums `$70F2/$70F3`): the same steps, with one asymmetry. When the primary checksum matches, the code copies primary→backup and sets only `$70F2`, then **falls through into the backup check** (`31:F18A`) instead of jumping on. If that second compare fails, slot 1 is wiped (`SaveSlot1Reset` `31:F1A3`). Slot 0 jumps straight on (`JMP $F140`).
3. **Current image** (`BootCheckCurrent` `31:F1E2`): `$7D00-$7FFF` must equal the mirror `$6D00-$6FFF` **and** carry the `AMI` tag at `$7DFD`. There is no checksum here. Otherwise → `CurSaveReset` `31:F219`.
   - Then `SetR7($7005)` and return (`31:F1C7`).

**Reset paths.** Each one sets `$7001`=`$FF`.
- `SaveSlot0Reset` / `SaveSlot1Reset`: write template page `23:BE00` to the primary and backup first page, and zero their other two pages. Checksums are not rewritten.
- `CurSaveReset`: write the template to `$7D00` and `$6D00`, and zero `$7E00/$7F00/$6E00/$6F00`.
- `ColdSaveInit` `31:F1CD` (cold boot only, when `$0182-$0184` ≠ `"USO"`; was `ColdInitSub`): clears `$700E/$700F`, then jumps to `CurSaveReset` **unconditionally**. So a power-on always starts with a fresh current image. Slots 0/1 survive; they are checked by `BootSaveCheck` right after.

**Checksum.** It is a plain 8-bit sum of 768 bytes (3 pages).
- `SaveChecksum` `23:BD92` starts from 0 and writes `$70F5` + copy `$70F4`. `MenuSave` copies `$70F5` to `$70F0/$70F1` (slot 0) or `$70F2/$70F3` (slot 1).
- **Seed quirk:** `SaveSumSlot` `31:F23D` never clears `$16`. The sum starts from whatever `$16` holds at boot (power-on RAM, or leftovers on reset; `Reset` does not clear it). The backup check also starts from the failed primary sum.
- So the repair path only works when `$16` happens to be 0 (primary) or the two sums cancel out. This path only runs when primary ≠ backup (a save interrupted between the two `16:9CE0` copies). A port that wants exact behaviour must keep `$16` live; a port that only wants sane behaviour can seed 0 (record it as a deviation).

**Boot copy quirk** (`CopySramBlock` `31:F3A2`): copies `$7D00`→`$6480` and `$7E00`→`$6580`. That is one page off from the save layout (`$7E00` = flags → `$6480`). So at the title screen `story_flags` holds the name/stats page and `npc_state` holds the flags. The only path into gameplay found so far is `30:C9DA` → `LoadGameAndInit` `23:BCDA`. It reloads both pages from `$7E00/$7F00`, so the bad copy is transient. *(s71: state 0 `MainStateNewGame` is the second path, and it zeroes both pages. Harmless; confirmed in the emulator, see "New-game vs continue path" below.)*

**New-game vs continue path (session 71).** There are two ways into gameplay (`main_state` 1), and neither uses the boot-copied pages:
- **NEW GAME:** title menu START with cursor `$82`=0 (`19:A77F`) → `title_mode`=1 (name entry) → `NameEntryDone` `19:A57C` → `JMP 19:A6F9` (`main_state`=0) → **`MainStateNewGame` `30:C957`**. It never reads the `23:BE00` template or `$7D00`. It initialises RAM directly: `$6428-$6477` = `$FF` (X=`$4F`), `story_flags` + `npc_state` = 0 (256-byte loop `30:C961`), `area_id`=0, `area_entry`=1, `GameStartObjs`, then xp_next=30, xp/gold/equipment/status/timers/cam = 0, `irq_mode`=0, **MP cur/max = `$22`** (34, `30:C9CE`), then `30:C008` (stat recalc). After that, `main_state`=1 and `sub_state`=0. `$6400-$6427` keep the name that name entry just wrote. **`$6478-$647F` are not initialised**; they keep whatever battery WRAM holds, and that is harmless because the shop code rewrites them before any read (below).
- **CONTINUE:** cursor `$82`=1 → `19:A78B` `main_state`=8 → `MainStateContinue` `30:C9DA` → `LoadGameAndInit` (`$7D00` image). On a power-on, `ColdSaveInit` has already reset `$7D00` to the template, so CONTINUE without a saved current image loads the template: area 0, x `$88`, y `$78`, HP `$30/$30`, name `------`.
- So the template is the "empty current image", not the new-game state. Both paths rewrite `$6480-$667F` (state 0 by zeroing, state 8 from `$7E00/$7F00`), so **the off-by-one boot copy `31:F3A2` is harmless**. **VERIFIED in tools.emu** (`python3 -m tools.emu.save_probe boot`, read watch on all of `$6480-$667F`):
  - NEW GAME (canonical keys): the boot copy runs at f214, `main_state`=0 at f2320, `MainStateNewGame` at f2321. The first read of the range is f2323 (`30:C066`), after state 0 has cleared it. There are 0 reads during f214-2321.
  - CONTINUE (`--keys "1250:START 1400:START 1500:SELECT 1600:START"`; the title menu, `title_mode` 6, is live f1420-1600): `main_state`=8 at f1600, `MainStateContinue` at f1601. The first read is f1603 (`30:C066`), after `LoadGameAndInit`. Then the reads are `31:E71A`/`E81D` (area load), and at f1629 `23:BCFB` (an autosave `SaveGame`, area 0 = `$80`).
- `$6410-$6415` = the **upper tile row of the name** (name entry `19:A3E4/A456/A562` writes it; `19:A4FD` reads it back). Readers: `PauseScreen` `15:BD01` copies `$6410+X` and `player_name6` `$6418+X` (X=5..0) to `wram_buf` `$6000/$6006`, which is queued as PPU block 9 (the two-row name on the status screen). The ending copies them at `17:A40C`, `$6420` chars, to `$6000/$6020`.
- **`$6470-$647F` = shop state** (bank 16): `ShopSetupArmor` `16:988A` / `ShopSetupTool` `16:98E3` first call `ShopIdForArea` `16:9953`, which looks `area_id` up in `ShopAreaTable` `16:9F54` (33 areas). The match index goes through `16:9F75` to `shop_id` `$646D` (0-10). On no match, `shop_id` = 33 (out of range; never happens for a placed shop). Then they copy 4 goods ids to `$6470-$6473` (armor `16:9DA4`, tool `16:9E28`, 4 bytes per shop, `$FF` = empty) and 4 price words to `$6478-$647F` (armor `16:9DD0`, tool `16:9E54`, 8 bytes per shop). `ShopPriceSel` `16:9A37` copies the price for cursor `$6427` (0 if the slot is `$FF`) into `$6474/$6475`.
  - The template's `$6478` words 30/40/60/50 are tool shop 0's prices (goods `1D 1E 24 31`), but its `$6470-$6477` are `$FF`. That is exactly what `MainStateNewGame` leaves (it fills `$6428-$6477` and stops short of `$6478`). So the template is a WRAM snapshot from a dev run: a tool-shop-0 visit, then a new game. Its `$6410` row is 6 × `$20` (blank upper-row tiles).
  - Example prices: armor shop 3 = 1800/1300/2000/9000, tool shop 10 = 1000/4000/3000/1500.

**SaveGame length quirk → NPC state is not saved.** Descriptor 1 (`$6480→$7E00`, count `FF 01`) was meant to copy 512 bytes (`$6480-$667F` = flags + `npc_state`). `BlockCopy` copies lo+1+hi = **257** bytes, so only `npc_state[0]` (`$6580` → `$7F00`) is saved. `$7F01-$7FFF` stay zero from the clear at `23:BC2B`. `LoadGameAndInit` then copies `$7F00`→`$6580`, so **every per-NPC state byte except NPC 0 resets to 0 on load**. **VERIFIED in tools.emu, s71** (`python3 -m tools.emu.save_probe npc`: from `area1_enter.state`, `npc_state` was filled with a non-zero pattern, then `SaveGame` `23:BC09` and `LoadGameAndInit` `23:BCDA` were called for real). With a write watch on `$7E00-$7FFF`, all 512 bytes are written, but the only non-zero one is `$7F00` (`npc_state[0]`). After the load, `npc_state[0]` comes back and 255/256 bytes are 0. `LoadGame` descriptor 1 has the same 257-byte length. Descriptor 4 (`$7D00→$6D00`, `FF 02`, meant to be 768) copies 258 bytes, but the explicit mirror loop at `23:BC65` copies all 768 anyway, so it does no harm. The port must reproduce the 257-byte copy.

Other boot finds: `boot_ab_flag` `$0F` = 1 if A+B are held at cold boot (`31:F38D`). *(s77 correction: it has one reader.)* **`DebugPauseCheat` `15:BF47`** tests it: with the flag set, pad-2 held `$44` low nibble (any direction) → every empty inventory slot (`$6430+X` b7 set, X=`$17`…0) is filled from `DebugItemTable` `15:BF7F` (24 item ids), `player_level`=`$0F`, `mp_cur`=`mp_max`=`$FF`; pad-2 A (`$44` b7) → slot 0/1 `obj_flags2` = 9 (speed 9). Its only caller is `PauseWait` `15:BDE4` (every frame of the status screen), but that `JSR` targets **`15:BF46`, a lone `RTS` just before the body**, so the cheat is switched off by one byte and never runs. The flag is still dead in practice.

#### Pointer tables + bank references (session 32, `tools/check_bank_refs.py` → `docs/bank_refs.txt`, `tools/find_ptr_tables.py` → `docs/ptr_tables.txt`)

Both tools reuse the `disasm_all.py` tracer (`check_bank_refs.build()`), including its `(r6, r7)` bank context per instruction.

**Bank-reference check** (exit 1 on any hard failure; **0 hard failures**):
- **calls**: 2057 `JSR/JMP abs` into `$8000-$FFFF`. 2052 land on a traced instruction start in a bank mapped at that window. 5 do not, and none is a real bug:
  - 4 are `16:9BC6/9BD6/9C4B/9C64` → `$BC00/$BC03`. The tracer assumes the default context 16/17, but bank 16 runs with **23** at `$A000` (`SaveJumpTable`, see Code coverage). *(s72: fixed with `BANK_CTX`; the check now gives 2383/2383 calls OK.)*
  - `15:AB84 JSR $9824` is unresolved: the bank is restored by `PLA/SetR6`, so it is `26:9824`.
- **tables**: 404 `.dw` entries (`JUMP_TABLES` + vectors). 365 are OK and 39 are `$0000` nulls (`ObjAiTable` 27, `SubstateHandlerTable` 10, `State3SubTable` 2).
- **banks**: 136 `SwitchBank16/SetR6/SetR7` calls. 112 have an `LDA #imm`, and every value is valid: SwitchBank16 n ≤ 14, SetR6/SetR7 ≤ 29. The 24 with a dynamic A are listed:
  - 17 are `PLA`/shadow restores (`RestoreBanks`, `30:C04F/C0F8/C208/C858`, `31:E756/F875`, …).
  - `30:CAD8` uses `SubstateBankTable`, `31:EF43` uses the screen id (`ScreenPagePtr`), and `30:C411` is the SwitchBank16 `ORA #1` path.
  - **Quirk, `15:AB4E`**: `JSR Rand64` / `JSR SetR6` maps a **random bank 0-63** (MMC3 wraps it to 0-31) at `$8000`. It then reads a word at `$8000`+Y (Y = random byte mixed with the object's position) into `$30/$31` as random noise. It restores the bank afterwards.
- **data**: 570 `abs/abs,X/abs,Y` operands into ROM.
  - 474 OK; 80 `MAPPER` are register writes.
  - 12 `OUTWIN` are valid **odd-window mappings**, not bugs:
    - `SetR7($14)` puts bank **20 at `$A000`** for `ShotTable` `20:BC00/BD00/BD80`.
    - `SetR6($0B)` puts bank **11 at `$8000`** for the 256-byte copy `$9E00,Y` → `wram_buf` `$6000` at `30:CCA4` (in `sub_30_CC4D`) and `19:BE5E`. The source is `11:BE00-BEFF`, now labelled `Wram6000InitTable`.
  - 4 `CODE-READ` (a load from the address of a traced instruction):
    - `SndOctaveTable` `24:842D` and `ItemRemapTable` `30:D40C` are the known base-offset quirks.
    - **`27:B2E7 ADC $B2F1,X`** adds the routine's own code bytes, indexed by the object slot, to `obj_meta_dir`. It works as a cheap per-slot pseudo-random offset (quirk).
    - **`27:A8A2`** = `AiDirTable_A8A2` (`08 08 09 07`, picked by Rand64&3). These are data bytes that the tracer decodes as code: the `BNE` at `A8A0` is always taken, but the tracer cannot prove it.
- **labels**: every ROM label must lie inside its bank's default window. **Fixed:** `EventTriggerTable` was mis-banked as `14:A17A`. `$A17A` is in **bank 15** (pair 14/15, mapped by SwitchBank16(7)), so the label and all REVERSE.md mentions are now `15:A17A`. `20:BC00 ShotTable` stays: it is the odd-window mapping above.
- **Filler correction:** the `$6000` page copy reads `11:BEE0-BEFF`, which was in the DEAD range `11:BEE0-BFFF`. The range now starts at `11:BF00` (`filler_scan.DEAD`, label `StaleFill11_BF00`). *(s72: the copied bytes at `$60E0-$60FF` are never read; see "Session 72 leftovers check" (b).)*

**Pointer-table catalogue.** These were found from consumers: an indexed `LDA tbl,R` followed by `tbl+1,R` (word tables), or a lo/hi pair stored to `k`/`k+1`. Counts are **upper bounds**: the walk stops at a non-pointer word, a code byte or the next label. "Elem" is code if at least half of the targets are traced instruction starts. `[JT]` = already in `disasm_all.JUMP_TABLES`.

These are not pointer tables. 42 indexed word/byte-pair reads hold fewer than 2 pointer-like entries: record fields such as `ShotTable`, `CollPairTable`, `HudElemTable` and `LevelXpTable`, coordinate pairs, and the sound field tables. In addition, `26:84BC/84CC/84DC` (`ObjStepVector` step bytes) and `30:CF47` are byte tables whose values only look like pointers.

**Orphan runs.** A run here is ≥4 words in one window with no traced consumer. There are 597 raw runs; 245 pass the label/ascending filter. Most are coincidences in metasprite, area-record and rectangle data. The plausible ones:
- `20:8000` ×511: the message pointer arrays, read through `(zp),Y`. Already known.
- `17:ABDC` ×32 (ascending, → `$9204-$9899`).
- `12:8006/8032/804E/8060/8074`: ascending runs → `$A0EA-$B0BD`.
- `24:9EB8`/`9EFB` and `25:B87F`/`B941` (→ `$8360-$8877`).
- `26:83B7/83D4`, `26:8015/8038`, `19:BAFB`, `17:AC99`, `17:B1A1`, `18:9664`, `15:B708` (→ `$F060-$F099`).

They are queued for a check.

**Session 72 leftovers check.**
- **(a) `ChestPresencePtrs` `14:9C82`** (was `ItemAuxPtrs`; `tools/chest_presence_dump.py` → `gfx/chest_presence.txt`). It is not an item table. `SpawnH2` `31:E375` (chest, p < `$80`, type `$0D`) maps bank 7 pair, sets `$23` = p and calls `ChestPresence` `14:85BF`. That routine reads the list `ChestPresencePtrs[p]` with `NpcFlagTest` (2-byte flag refs, b0 b5 = invert, b7 = last) and returns `$20` = 1 on the first true condition (an **OR** list). With `$20` = 0, `SpawnH2` clears `obj_active`, so the chest never appears. The count is exactly **114** (p `$00-$71`): entry `$72` reads `00 01`, the start of the next data. The records are 58 two-byte refs at `15:A106-$A179` and end exactly at `EventTriggerTable` `15:A17A`. Every record is a single `!Fxxx`. The flag is the one the chest's item sets in `ItemGetTable`. I checked this against `gfx/items.txt` for three chests only: p `$1D` Medical Herb `!F0AA`, `$2F` Leather Boots `!F0E7` and `$03` Sword of Thunder `!F05F`. So a chest is hidden once its item has been taken. Unused params and the trap chest `$70` share `15:A110` = `!F000`; F000 is never set, so those chests are always present (the trap chest respawns). `$2B` has its own `!F000` copy at `A176`.
- **(a) `MenuSprShapes` `16:8B1F`** (was `MenuPtrTable_8B1F`): **4** entries (`$8B27 $8B38 $8B3D $8B4E`), and the first record starts right after the table. The consumer is `MenuSprDraw` `16:86D3`, with 7 callers in bank 16: `852F` (shape 1), `868C`/`8696` (3/2), `86AC` (0), `9834`, `9867` (2), `9CA6` (3). Inputs: `$12` = shape, `$2E` = first OAM slot − 1 (it writes from OAM byte `$2E·4+4`), and `wram_60F8`/`60F9`/`60FA`/`60FB` = Y offset / tile offset / attribute OR / X offset. A record is a count byte followed by that many `y tile attr x` sprites, each added to the offsets (the attribute is ORed). The shapes:
  - 0: 4 copies of tile +`$FF`, with no flip / V / H / HV flip, at (0,−1) (0,15) (16,−1) (16,15). These are the corners of a frame cursor.
  - 1: one tile +`$FE` at (0,−1), the arrow cursor.
  - 2: tiles +0..3 as a 2×2 block at (4,3)-(12,11), a 16×16 icon.
  - 3: tiles +0..3 in a row, 8 px apart, at y −1 (a 32×8 strip).
  The records end at `16:8B5E`. `86C5` reads the next table, `16:8B74`.
- **(a) `SceneMetaPtrs` `18:8F3B`** (was `State3PtrTable_8F3B`; consumer `SceneMetaDraw` `18:80E7`, s69): exactly **11** entries. `18:8F51` is a `$FF` pad, and the first record `$8F52` follows it. A record starts with 2 bytes → `$06C0/$06C1`, then 3-byte frames indexed by `$73`. Ids come from the scene scripts (`18:81FA`/`824F`, `(zp_8E),Y`) and from immediates `$10` (`18:82D1`, so not a `SceneMetaDraw` id) and 1/2 in bank 19 (`19:AA3A/AACE/AB07`). Bank 19 also writes `$72` for other uses.
- **(a) bank 20 tables = `WakeCutscene` `20:9400` (s38) data, not text.**
  - **`WakeSprFramePtrs` `20:9767`** has exactly **34** entries: the first target `$97AB` is the table end, and the last target is `$9A51`. Entries 0-7 and 8-15 are the same 8 frames in reverse order (an animation that plays there and back). `WakeSprFrame` `20:973D` (A = frame, 12 callers `20:958E-96EA`) jumps to `WakeSprDraw` `20:9500`.
  - **`WakeSprDraw` format:** a count, then per sprite `dy tile attr dx`. `dy` = `$80` puts the sprite off-screen (`$F8`). Y is `dy` + `wram_60FC`. X is `dx` + `wram_60FF`; a carry out (or a negative `dx` that wraps) hides the sprite. OAM writes start at `$2A`.
  - **`WakePalPtrs` `20:9A6A`** has **12** entries pointing to 16-byte BG palette sets at `$9A82-$9B01`. The sequence is `$9A92 $9AA2 $9AB2 $9AC2 $9AB2 $9AA2 $9A92 $9A82` and `$9AF2 $9AE2 $9AD2 $9AC2`, i.e. fades. `20:94C9` (X = `$20`, already a byte offset) copies the set to `pal_buf` and copies its colour 0 into `$6150/54/58/5C` (the sprite palettes' backdrop entries), after an `$21`-frame wait.
- **(a) `EquipNameBase` `15:BEB7` is real, not a mis-trace** (`tools/equip_name_dump.py`). The consumer `EquipNameRow` `15:BE7A` starts 1 byte after the end of a menu string, which explains the odd decode at `BE78`. Its only caller is the status screen loop `15:BD51-BD6F`. For slot `$18` = 0-3 that loop does three things: it copies a 4-byte queue header from `15:BDCB`+slot·4 to `$600C`, calls `EquipNameRow`, then calls `PpuQueueBlock` with `$17` = `$0B`+slot.
  - `EquipNameRow` does `SetR6(6)`, so bank 6 is at `$8000` and the tracer's context 06 was right. The row address is `EquipNameBase[slot]` + `$0711[slot]`·`$100`; an empty slot uses `$80F0` (16 spaces). It copies 16 bytes to `$6010-$601F`, then restores `SetR6($1A)`.
  - The bases are `$80F0` swords, `$85F0` **magic** 1-8, `$8EF0` armor and `$97F0` shields. So the status screen's 4 rows are `$0711` sword, `$0712` magic, `$0713` armor and `$0714` shield; the accessory `$0716` is not shown here.
  - The names are ASCII-like 16-byte rows at `$xxF0` of each bank-6 page. Multi-tile glyphs are used for "Sword of" (`0A 0B 0C 5C 5D`), "Armor" (`7B 11 12`) and "Shield" (`0D 0E 0F`).
  - The bases overlap: id 9 of each class reads the next class's blank base row, and shield id 9 would read `$A0F0`, which is in the R7 window rather than bank 6. That should not happen, because the rows show at most 5 swords and 8 of each other class. I did not check the equip code's limits.
- **(b) Who reads the `Wram6000InitTable` page** (`tools/ppu_block_dump.py`). `PpuQueueBlock` `30:C482` takes 5-byte records at `30:C518`+id·5: PPU hi, PPU lo, length, source offset into `$6000`, rows (b7 = no `WaitPpuQueue`). Per row the PPU address advances by max(len, `$20`) and the source by len. There are exactly **70** blocks (`$00-$45`); the table ends at `$C675`, right before `WaitPpuQueue` `30:C676`. The page copied from `11:BE00` is the tile source for two screen rebuilds:
  - `SubstRestoreScreen` `30:CC4D` queues blocks `$3E-$43` right after the copy. Their sources are `$6000-$6093`: four 6-wide window pieces (2 or 4 rows each), an 8×8 block at `$20CC` and a 4×3 block at `$21CE`. The `96 97 … 98` / `BD BD … BE` bytes are their frame tiles.
  - `19:BE5E` queues block `$45` (`$60D4-$60DF`, a 4×3 block at `$218E`).
  - **A third copy site, `27:BCFC`**, was missed in session 32. An object AI step (`INC obj_phase,X` at `27:BCF6`, which is in the AI `$70` final-scene phase code `Ai70PhaseDispatch` `27:BA2E`) does its own `SetR6($0B)` + 256-byte copy and then queues block **`$44`** (`$6094-$60D3`, a second 8×8 at `$20CC`; it replaces block `$42`'s picture), followed by `SetR6($1A)`. So `$6000-$60DF` is used in full by blocks `$3E-$45`.
  - **`$60E0-$60FF` (`11:BEE0-BEFF`) is never read from the copy.** No block covers it. The only code that reads it is the bank 16 menus (`$60E0,Y` `16:8589`, `$60EC,X` `16:8626`, `MenuSprDraw` offsets `$60F8-$60FB`) and `WakeSprDraw` (`$60F8/$60FC/$60FF`), and all of those write the bytes themselves first in the same routine family. So the 32 copied bytes (`00 01 … 1F` with a few changes) are dead, and `filler_scan.DEAD` could start at `11:BEE0` again. I did not change it, because the copy does read those bytes.
  - Blocks `$05`/`$06` (len 96 × 3 rows) run their source offset past `$FF`. The source byte is 8-bit (`$23`), so the offset wraps to `$6000`.
- **(c) Orphan runs** (`tools/orphan_check.py`). For each run it searches for abs operands into the run, `LD? #lo` / `LD? #hi` pairs and `.dw` words. It only keeps hits in the run's own 16 KB pair and in the fixed banks 30/31, because without that filter there are hundreds of hits in unrelated banks. I checked every remaining hit by disassembling it.
  - **`15:B708` is a real table, at `15:B709`: `BossBgStripPtrs`.** The run was off by one byte: `B708` is an `RTS`. Its consumer is `BossBgStrip` `15:B6C1`, called from `15:B377`/`B4CB` (AI `$EB` phase code, `AiBowOfTruth9E` family). With A = n (0-9) it reads the pointer `$80F0`+n·`$300` and does `SetR6(7)`. It then copies `$C0` bytes from the `$xxF0-$xxFF` row of consecutive bank-7 pages into `$6000`. At each page change X backs off by 2, so 14 bytes are kept per page. Finally it does `SetR6($1A)` and `PpuQueueBlock($2F+n)` (14 × 3 tiles, no wait). Blocks `$2F-$33` and `$34-$38` place strips at the same 5 PPU rows, so n = 0-4 and 5-9 are **two 14×15-tile BG pictures** of the boss (two animation frames, `equip_name_dump.py --bank7`). Only the first 42 copied bytes are queued; the rest of the `$C0` copy is overcopy. Banks 6 and 7 therefore share a layout: the last 16 bytes of every page hold a name or tile row.
  - **`12:8006/8032/804E/8060/8074` are inside `SfxPtrTable` `12:8000`** (88 SFX header pointers, mapped at `$A000`; already decoded). They are not separate tables.
  - **No consumer** for `17:ABDC`, `24:9EB8`/`9EFB`, `25:B87F`/`B941`, `26:8015/8038/83B7/83D4`, `19:BAFB`, `17:AC99`, `17:B1A1` or `18:9664`. The in-range hits (`30:C271` = `ObjTypeTable`, `26:9AE5` = `BitMask_9AE0` bytes, `30:C005`/`31:EC84`/`12:952E` = data or other-pair code, `31:F325` = `JSR $8020` under another bank pair) are all false. The contents look like byte data, not pointers: `92 93` tile pairs (`17:ABDC`), ascending tile ids `80 81 82 …` (`17:AC99`), `77 xx` byte pairs (`24:9EB8`) and `ObjStepVector`-like byte tables (bank 26). Two kinds of consumer are not covered: `(zp),Y` reads through runtime pointers built from bank-relative arithmetic, and odd-window mappings. I consider these runs closed as data.
- **(d) Tracer fixes in `tools/disasm_all.py`:**
  - `BANK_CTX = {16: (16, 23)}` sets the default context for bank 16 seeds, and an instruction's stored context is replaced when the bank's own context reaches it. Bank 16's `$BC00/$BC03` calls now disassemble as `SaveJumpTable`/`sub_23_BC03`.
  - `27:A8A2-A8A5` is in `KNOWN_DATA` (`AiDirTable_A8A2`). The only warning left is the expected `27:A86C -> 27:A8A2 runs into data`: the fall-through of the always-taken `BNE` at `A8A0`.
  - `DATA_NAME_RE` now also accepts the suffixes `Shapes|Base|Rows|Goods|Prices`. Without them the s71 shop labels `16:9DA4/9DD0` and the new `MenuSprShapes`/`EquipNameBase` were seeded as code.
  - Rerun results: the round-trip is OK for all 32 banks, with 0 rejected and 0 unresolved targets. `check_bank_refs.py` reports **2383/2383 calls OK** (session 32 had 5 misses), CODE-READ 3 (`27:A8A2` gone) and OUTWIN 13 (the new one is the `27:BD03` copy from (b)). Coverage changed in bank 15 (3384 → 3456 code; `EquipNameRow` is now traced), bank 16 (unknown 699 → 175) and bank 30 (+350 data from `PpuBlockTable`).

### State Machine

Main state `$40` — 6 states, see MainLoop. Session 22: state 2 = init (`18:9FCC` → `19:A000`, ends `INC main_state`), state 3 = per-frame `19:A015` with sub-modes `$06F3`/`$06F0` (see "Full-ROM disassembly").

**Summary (session 48, full table in `docs/architecture_exe.md` §2.2):** 2 (boot/title init) → 3 (title menu, story, status, name entry; step 6 writes 0) → 0 `30:C957` (new-game init: clears `$6428-$6477` to `$FF`, story/NPC flags `$6480/$6580`, player stats; `$6C`=0, `$6D`=1; → state 1, substate 0) → 1 `30:CAB6` (gameplay, substate `$41` via `SubstateHandlerTable` `30:CADE` + `SubstateBankTable` `30:CB2E`; substate 8 = `SubstPlay`). States 4/5 (`17:A000`/`A100`) never ran in any emulator run. **`SubstateBankTable` full (s48):** n=9 for substates 3, 24-27 and 30 (handlers `19:B900`, `19:BB39 BB9D BC04 BC6B`, `19:BDF2`), n=7 for 12/13 (`15:BC40`/`15:BDDB`), n=0 for the rest. New labels: `PpuQueueFlush` `30:C67D` (NMI ring flush of `$6200`), `SubstPlayTail` `31:E0B8` (every 16 frames one spawn slot `$0D`+(`$08`>>4) is re-checked and, if empty, the area record is re-read — a respawn tick; static RE only).

**Main states (session 63, static; `MainStateTable` `30:C91F` = 9 words, not 6).** Every `STA/INC/DEC $40` in the ROM (`search_bytes 85 40 / e6 40 / c6 40`, misaligned hits re-checked from aligned starts) is listed as a setter.

| `$40` | entry | name | what it does | set by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `30:C957` | `MainStateNewGame` | new-game init (clears inventory `$6428-$6477`, flags, stats; `$6D`=1) then `30:C977` → 1 | title step 6 `19:A6FB` (after name entry) |
| 1 | `30:CAB6` | `MainStateGameplay` | substate dispatcher `$41` (`SubstateHandlerTable`) | `30:C977` (new game, substate 0), `30:C9F5` (continue, substate 1) |
| 2 | `30:C931` | `MainStateTitleInit` | `SwitchBank16(9)` → `18:9FCC` `MainState2Init` → `19:A000` (`INC $40` at `19:A00D`) | Reset `31:F333`; `PlayerDeath` `19:B9AD` (game over → title, after `JSR 19:BA0F`); `DEC $40` at `19:A796` (state 3 → re-init title) |
| 3 | `30:C939` | `MainStateTitle` | `ReadPadsMenu` + `19:A015` (title menu / story / status / name entry, see "Main states 2/3") | `INC` `19:A00D` |
| 4 | `30:C944` | `MainStateEndingInit` | `SwitchBank16(8)` → `17:A000` `EndingInit`: `17:A1A2`, `A744`, both nametables cleared to `$FF`/attr `$55` (`NtFill_b17`), `A788`, `irq_mode`=3, scroll/camera 0, `$060E $0600-$0602 $0639`=0, `17:AA22`(A=0 / `$80`), `$063D`=`$F0`, `$063E`=4, `INC $40` → 5, `$700E`=`$FF` | **only** `19:BFC4` = the end of `SubstFinalSceneReset` `19:BDF2` (substate 30): a self-timed final cut-scene (player walks to x=`$80`, messages via `EventMsgTable19` `19:BFC9`, scroll until slot-14 y = `$E0`), then `JSR 19:8C3D` (A=`$20`), `main_state`=4 |
| 5 | `30:C94C` | `MainStateEnding` | `ReadPadsMenu` + `17:A100` `EndingTick`: `frame_ready`=0, `DEC $8C` (frame counter), mode `$0602` via `EndingModeTable` `17:A11A`: 0 = `EndingScene` `17:A14A` (if `$060E`≠0: table `17:A140` [1-4] = `A056 A069 A0A2 A0BC`; else per-phase table `EndingPhaseTable` `17:A166` on `obj_phase` `$0600`, ≥ 20 entries, `17:A0EB` = shared wait step: `$0604` 60-frame tick × `$0605` count, then `INC $0600`), 1 = `EndingFadeIn` `17:AA74` (every 8 frames `pal_buf` +`$10` per entry until all 32 equal the target `$0618-$0637`, then mode 0), 2 = `EndingFadeOut` `17:AAAD` (every 4 frames −`$10` per entry, floor `$0F`) | `INC` `17:A04E` |
| 6, 7 | `$0000` | — | null (would `JMP $0000`) | never set |
| 8 | `30:C9DA` | `MainStateContinue` | `GameStartObjs` `30:C9FF`, `SetR7($17)` + `LoadGameAndInit` `23:BC06`, `WaitPpuQueue`, `30:C008`, `SwitchBank16(9)` + common screen setup `19:BA65`, then `main_state`=1, `sub_state`=1 (`SubstAreaEnter`), `pal_nofade_mask`=0 | `19:A78D`: title START with `$82`=1 |

- **Title menu choice `$82` (`title_menu_sel`).** `19:A749` (title step after the logo): SELECT (`$20`) flips `$82` 0↔1 and moves the cursor sprite (`$02F3` = `19:A77B`[`$82`] = x `$40` / `$80`); START (`$10`) → `19:A77D`: `A07C`, `$06B2`=9, then `$82`≠0 → `main_state`=8 (continue), else `title_mode`=1 (new game: the story / status / name-entry chain that ends in state 0). With no input, `$06B4`/`$06B5` count down and `18:8344` + `INC title_step` runs. **Idle path (s83):** the menu entry `19:A6C8` sets `$06B4`=`$96`, `$06B5`=2, so after 150 + 256 = **406 frames** (≈ 6.8 s) without START the menu calls `TitleFadeOutStart` `18:8344` (`state3_mode`=2) and goes to step 2 `TitleMenuRestart` `19:A796`: `DEC main_state` → 2 = `MainState2Init` again, i.e. the opening replays (attract loop). A SELECT frame skips the decrement but does not reload the timer. Already in the web port (`states/title.js` `TitleMenuInput`).
- **No exit from the ending.** Nothing in banks 16/17 writes `$40` except the `INC` at `17:A04E`; states 4/5 are entered once and the game stays in state 5 until reset. States 4/5 never ran in any emulator run (the canonical script does not reach the ending), so this is static only.
- `GameStartObjs` `30:C9FF` (shared by states 0 and 8): `obj_active[0..31]`=0, `ppumask_shadow`=0, `irq_mode`=0, `WaitFrame`, then `ObjInit` for the 4 fixed slots from `30:CA26` (slot, type) pairs read backwards: (3,`$0A`) (0,`$06`) (2,`$05`) (1,`$04`).
- Correction to the "Boot copy quirk" note: `30:C9DA` is not the only way into gameplay — state 0 `30:C957` (new game) also enters state 1 without `LoadGameAndInit`.

#### Ending sequence, bank 17 (session 83, static RE + **self-emu checked**)

**Emulator run (s83, `python3 -m tools.emu.ending_probe` → screenshots `gfx/ending/step_NN.png`, `script_XX.png`, `final.png`):** poking `$40`=4 at f2454 from `area1_enter.state` runs the whole ending as traced below: `$40` 4 → 5 at f2456 (`17:A050`; no other `$40` write afterwards), step 29 reached at **f8716 (≈ 6,260 frames ≈ 104 s)**, then the screen stays. Sound requests `$4A` (f2550, step 2), `$5B` (f2654, end of step 3), `$1D` (f2916, step 8), `$1C` (f4051, step 15). Step times: 0-2 f2457-2550, scroll 3 f2551-2654 (104 frames: 44 rows + 40-frame flash + fade), 4-6 → f2878, epilogue 7-10 f2878-3640 (~13 s), page 2 11-13 → f3999, STAFF card 14-17 → f4173, staff roll 18-21 f4174-7643 (9 cards, ~330-340 frames each, as `docs/ending.txt` predicts), thanks 22-24 → f8011, final 25-28 → f8716. Pictures: step 3 = a tower rising out of a saucer-shaped base (the tile column + picture `17:ABDB`), the epilogue shows the player name as spaced 2-row tiles, the staff cards sit in a pink/green border box beside a night-scene portrait, step 28's metasprite 7 = the "THE END" logo over the final landscape with the two characters (metasprites 5/6).

**`EndingPhaseTable` `17:A166` has exactly 30 entries**: it ends where the code `17:A1A2` starts (`(A1A2-A166)/2` = 30). `obj_phase` `$0600` = step number. `A0EB` = `EndingWait` (60 frames × (`$0605`+1), reload `$3B`), used as steps 1 4 6 9 12 16 20 23 27.

| step | addr | step | addr | step | addr |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `A1BC` | 10 | `A4A8` | 20 | `A0EB` |
| 1 | `A0EB` | 11 | `A483` | 21 | `A603` |
| 2 | `A209` | 12 | `A0EB` | 22 | `A613` |
| 3 | `A212` | 13 | `A4A8` | 23 | `A0EB` |
| 4 | `A0EB` | 14 | `A4AF` | 24 | `A657` |
| 5 | `A38F` | 15 | `A58E` | 25 | `A696` |
| 6 | `A0EB` | 16 | `A0EB` | 26 | `A6D8` |
| 7 | `A3CB` | 17 | `A4EC` | 27 | `A0EB` |
| 8 | `A47A` | 18 | `A4F3` | 28 | `A6FB` |
| 9 | `A0EB` | 19 | `A597` | 29 | `A71B` = bare `RTS` → **the ending stops here for good** (step 29 never `INC`s; state 5 runs it every frame until reset) |

Bank 17 helpers (all named in labels.csv, s83):
- `A1A2` `EndingSilence` = `EndingSnd`(0) + `EndingPalBlack`. `A1AB` `EndingSnd`: A → `snd_queue` `$0103`, count `$0100`=1 under `snd_queue_lock` `$0101` (same protocol as the game's sound request).
- `AA17` `EndingPalBlack`: `pal_buf` ×32 = `$0F`. `AA22` `EndingPalLoad`(A): b7 = sprite half (X=`$10`), low nibble = palette number → 16 bytes from `EndingPalettes` `17:AAED` + 16·n into the fade target `$0618`/`$0628`, then copies `$0618` (BG colour 0) into `$0628/$062C/$0630/$0634`. `AA55` `EndingPalApply`: target → `pal_buf` at once. `AA61` `EndingFadeInStart`: `pal_buf` = target `AND $0F` (darkest row), `$0602`=1. `AAA7` `EndingFadeOutStart`: `$0602`=2.
- `A720`/`A728` NMI off/on (`PPUCTRL` b7), `A730`/`A73A` rendering off/on (`PPUMASK` `AND $E1`/`ORA $1E`), `A744` `EndingWaitFrame` (spins on `frame_ready`; the in-step waits use it, so one "step" call can span many frames), `A788` `OamHide_b17` (all OAM y=`$F0`; keeps sprite 0 unless `irq_mode` is 0 or 3).
- **Data formats** (decoded by `tools/ending_dump.py` → `docs/ending.txt`; every data pointer is a ptr16 *variable* in bank 17 that holds the block address, e.g. `($A4E3)` = `A4E5`). **The ending font is ASCII-coded** (`$20` space, `A-Z`, `.`/`,`/`&`/`'`; `_` is the full stop glyph). `A81C` `EndingPicture`: `W H` (metatiles), W·H metatile numbers → 4 tiles each from `17:B004/B0C4/B184/B244` (TL/TR/BL/BR, 192 entries), two chained queue rows per metatile row, one frame per row; then 64 attribute bytes (`$23C0` of the `$8B` table), 32 bytes fade target `$0618`, 2 bytes → `chr_r0_play/r1` `$07F0`, 4 bytes → `$07F2-$07F5`; the pointer `$88` is left at the next block (the staff pictures are one stream at `17:AC85`). `AB1D` `EndingTextLines`: `W H`, then H × (W tiles + 1 byte = address advance, `$20/$40/$60` = 1-3 rows), one line per frame. `A7A0` `EndingRect`: `W H` + W·H tiles.
- `A7A0` (and `AB1D`) queue at `$84/$85` = start `$8A/$8B`; `$0639` is ORed into the queue record's hi byte (b6 = PPU +32 increment, so `$0639`=`$40` blits columns, +1 per record), else rows (+`$20`). `A7A0` waits one frame per row/column. (Queue hi byte: b7 = chain the next record in the same NMI, b6 = +32 increment, `world/ppuqueue.js`.)

**Step 0 `A1BC`** (setup): `A81C` blits the rectangle at `($ABD9)` to `$2000`, text pointer `$88` = `($B854)`, text row `$78/$79` = `$20C0`, blank row `$7A/$7B` = `$2800`, line count `$063A`=0, `$70`=`$F0`, `$71`=`$5C`, wait 0/1, `EndingFadeInStart`. **Step 2 `A209`**: `EndingSnd($4A)` (the ending music). **Step 3 `A212`** = a **vertical scroll past a tall tile column** (not text: the list `17:B856` is 32 records of 1-4 tiles `$F0-$FB`/`$FF` at columns 12-15, `docs/ending.txt`), one row per frame: record = `col, tiles…, 0` (a first byte of 0 = end of list → `A337`); the line goes to the queue at `$78/$79`+col (len X, src `$6000`, hi b7 = chained with) a blank `$FF`×32 row at `$7A/$7B` (src `$6020`); both row pointers step +`$20` and wrap `$23C0` → `$2800` / `$2BC0` → `$2000` (two stacked nametables = 60 rows). At line `$18` the `$23C0` attributes are set to `$55`. From line `$14` on, `cam_y` += 10 per line (wraps at `$F0` and flips `cam_y_hi`); from line `$20` on, `$70` −= 4 and `A94B`(A=8) runs. At the end of list: pointer reset to `($B854)`; `SBC #$2C` runs with C = 0 (every entry path clears it), so if `$063A` < `$2D` it loops straight back into `A212` in the same frame. The test only runs at the end of the list, so the 32-record list is read **twice = 64 rows** (correction s90: not 44; the s83 emulator time of 104 frames = 64 rows + 40 flash frames). Otherwise `$8D`=`$3C`, fade-out, wait 0/1, then 8 × (`EndingPalLoad(1)` + apply + 2 frames, `EndingPalLoad(2)` + apply + 3 frames) = a **flashing palette** (40 frames), `EndingSnd($5B)`, next step.

Sprites: `A94B` `EndingSprRel`(A) and `A9D7` `EndingSprAbs`(A) draw metasprite A from the pointer table `EndingSprites` `17:B45E` into OAM from `$063E` up (first *every byte* of OAM from `$063E` to `$02FF` is set to `$F0`, not just the y bytes, s90). Record = `attr, dx, dy, tile`, `$FF` ends. `Rel` adds signed dx to `$71` (x) and dy to `$70` (y), drops a sprite that wraps off-screen or has y ≥ `$063D` (the `BCC $A9BA` lands inside the `JMP` = skip trick); `Abs` uses dx/dy as screen x/y. So in the ending `$70`/`$71` are the y/x of one hero metasprite, not slot-0 `obj_x_lo`.

- **Step 5 `A38F`**: metasprite 8 (rel); `$70` += 1 every 4 frames (moves down) while `$8D` (=`$3C` from step 3) counts down; then at the next 8-frame boundary one `EndingFadeOut` call per frame until the palette is black (`$0602` is set to 2 just for that call and back to 0), wait 0/1.
- **Scene order** (from `docs/ending.txt`): step 0-6 the scroll scene (16×4-metatile picture `17:ABDB`, CHR `2C 34 10-13`, hero metasprite 8 rising); 7-10 the epilogue page 1 (9 lines of text with the **player's name** in line 7, before "& <partner>"); 11-13 epilogue page 2 (6 lines); 14-17 the "STAFF" title card; 18-21 the **staff roll** = 9 pages (`EndingScriptBytes`: 5 portrait pictures 5×15 metatiles at `$2004` + metasprites 0-4, each with 1-2 role/name cards 16×8 at `$2250`, ~332-338 frames per card, sub-effect 1 fade-all-in on a new portrait, 2 = fade in the card only, 3/4 = fade out); 22-24 the "special thanks" card (17×11); 25-28 the final picture (16×15 metatiles `17:B306`, CHR `1C 54 2C 2D 7E 7F`) with metasprites 5/6/9/7; 29 = end.
- **Step 7 `A3CB`** (name screen): `chr_r0_play` `$07F0`=`$3C`, `cam_y`=0, NT `$2000` filled with `$10`/attr 0, OAM hidden, black; target `$0619`=`$30`; `AB1D` with `($BE37)` at `$20C4`; then the **player name**: `$6420` = name length (from `$6410..` → `$6000`, `$6418..` → `$6020`, Y=`$6420` bytes), centred with `$7E` = 6 − length: two queue records `$22`/`$08+$7E` (hi `$A2` = chained) and `$22`/`$28+$7E`, then fade in, wait 2/12 (≈ 13 s).
- **Step 8 `A47A`**: `EndingSnd($1D)`. **Step 10/13 `A4A8`**: `EndingFadeOutStart`. **Step 11 `A483`**: NT `$2000` = `$10`, `AB1D` at `$20C4` (next page of the same `$88` stream), fade in, wait 0/5.
- **Step 14 `A4AF`**: `chr_r0_play`=`$1C`, NT = `$20`, the 5×1 rectangle at `($A4E3)` = **"STAFF"** (tiles are ASCII) blitted at `$21CE`, fade in, wait 0/2. **Step 15 `A58E`**: `EndingSnd($1C)`. **Step 17 `A4EC`**: fade out.
- **Step 18 `A4F3`** (staff-roll frame): NT = `$2A`, attr `$FF`; the rectangle `($AB95)` is blitted 4 times: as columns at `$2003` and `$200E` (`$0639`=`$40`) and as rows at `$2220` and `$2340` (a border box); the `($A57A)` 2×8 blank rectangle at `$2240`; `$88` = `($AC83)` (the staff text stream), script index `$8D`=0, OAM hidden.
- **Step 19 `A597`** = `EndingScript` interpreter on the byte list `EndingScriptBytes` `17:A65E` (index `$8D`): `b` = next byte; **b7 set** → fade out and `obj_phase`=`$16` (step 22, end of the roll); **0** → `A81C` blits the next rectangle of the `($88)` stream at `$2004`, the next byte = metasprite (`EndingSprAbs`), loop in the same frame; **1-127** → rectangle number `b` from `17:B8D2` (ptr16 ×b) blitted at `$2250` (`$88` saved/restored), then 3 bytes: `$060E` (palette sub-effect), `$0605`, `$0604` (wait), → step 20 (wait) → **step 21 `A603`**: `$060E` = next byte, `obj_phase` = `$13` (back to step 19). So each staff page = text from the stream + a picture + a fade in → wait → fade out.
- **Step 22 `A613`**: black, fade target all `$0F`, OAM hidden, NT = `$20`, target `$0619`=`$20`, rectangle `($BD78)` at `$2128` (one row per frame), fade in, wait `$14`/5. **Step 24 `A657`**: fade out.
- **Step 25 `A696`** (final picture): rectangle `($B304)` at `$2000` (full screen), metasprites 5 and 6 (abs) at OAM `$8C`/`$AC` (`$063E`), then `$71`=`$74`, `$70`=`$14`, clip y `$063D`=`$78`, metasprite 9 (rel), fade in. **Step 26 `A6D8`**: metasprite 9 (rel) moves down 1 px every 4 frames until y = `$8C` (clipped at y `$78` = it sinks behind the picture's lower edge), wait `$28`/2. **Step 28 `A6FB`**: target `$0635-$0637` = `$20 $24 $04`, apply at once, metasprite 7 (abs) at OAM `$8C`. **Step 29**: `RTS` forever.
- `$060E` sub-effects (`EndingSubTable`, run every 4 frames *instead of* the step, `17:A120` `EndingSubRun`: `EndingScene` `A14A` only dispatches the step when `$060E` = 0, so a staff card's wait starts once its fade is done (s90); each ends by writing 0 to `$060E` when its count `$0638` is complete): 1 `A056` fade all 32 entries in by `$10` per run (`A07C` keeps the target's low nibble); 2 `A069` fade in entries 9-11 only (BG palette 2, colours 1-3 = the picture); 3 `A0A2` fade out entries 0-11 and 16-31 (all but BG palette 3) to `$0F`; 4 `A0BC` fade out entries 9-11.

A summary of this file; REVERSE.md stays the source of truth |
| `docs/architecture_web.md` | **session 49**: web-port design. Rules: game state in RAM/WRAM arrays at the ROM addresses (so the `--compare` CSV and the save image work unchanged), no hand-copied tables, no bank switching except CHR R0-R5, DOM-free game logic, generator functions for routines that wait across frames. Has the JS module graph, an EXE→web mapping table (ROM routine → JS function/module), the state machine and frame flow, the `web/data/` file table and the verification plan. Status of every module: planned |

See "Full-ROM disassembly" under Key Findings. `--labels` prints how each ROM label is classified (code/data by name: `DATA_NAME_RE`, `CODE_NAMES`). Its output is checked by a built-in 2-pass assembler that re-assembles every file and compares it with the ROM; the run fails if any byte differs |
| `disasm/bank_NN.asm` | 32 files, one per 8KB bank, ca65-like syntax: `.org` = CPU window (bank 22 at `$A000`); `NAME = $XXXX` equates for symbols outside the file; `a:` forces absolute mode on operands below `$100`; jump tables as `.dw name`; data as `.db` (16 per line, split at labels); each code line ends with `; AAAA  raw bytes`. Auto-names are `sub_BB_AAAA` (JSR / table targets) and `loc_BB_AAAA` (branch / JMP targets). An instruction that runs over a window edge is emitted as `.db` with a `-- continues in the next window` comment |
| `disasm/coverage.txt` | code / data / unknown bytes per bank, round-trip status, auto-name count, auto table counts, the 33 indirect `JMP (…)` sites, unresolved `$8000-$BFFF` targets, rejected targets, and warnings |
